{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CdQGqwuGdpaFJBBFZL4hi9","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Validate tool input against the registered inputSchema\n2\t\n3\tIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#37\n4\tLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:M, area:tools, epic:extension-points\n5\t\n6\tPart of #35\n7\t\n8\t## Why\n9\t\n10\t`ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\n11\twithout ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\n12\tEvery tool declares a JSON Schema (`src/types/ai.ts`) that is handed verbatim to both\n13\tproviders, but nothing validates what comes back from the model. The shipped example shows the\n14\tshape of the problem: `example/weather-tool.ts` declares `location` as a required string, then\n15\tcasts and defaults it (`(input.location as string) || 'Unknown'`) with a comment warning the reader\n16\tthat `inputSchema` shapes what the model sends but does not enforce it. That is the careful version,\n17\twritten by the people who wrote the engine — and it still answers a malformed call with a\n18\tconfidently formatted forecast for `'Unknown'` rather than telling the model it got the call wrong.\n19\t\n20\tThe registry currently pushes this check onto each tool's own executor, leaving every consumer\n21\tto implement it itself — differently each time, or not at all. A tool that receives unexpected input must fail\n22\tin one predictable, known way before the rest of this epic's tool work can be built on top\n23\tof it.\n24\t\n25\t## What to do\n26\t\n27\tValidate `input` against the resolved tool definition's `inputSchema` inside\n28\t`ToolRegistry.execute`, before the executor runs, using a JSON Schema validator (`ajv`\n29\tunless the build proves otherwise) added to `dependencies` — not a second, hand-written\n30\tschema in a different format, since the schema already has to match what is handed to the\n31\tproviders.\n32\t\n33\tCompile a static tool's schema once at `register` time so a broken schema fails at startup,\n34\tnot mid-conversation; a schema produced by a function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource`\n35\tresolves and compiles per call, cached by the resolved schema object.\n36\t\n37\tA rejection returns a `ToolResponse` (`{result: '<what was wrong>'}`) rather than throwing,\n38\tso a malformed call becomes something the model can read and correct within the existing\n39\t`DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5` budget, instead of ending the whole conversation with a\n40\tgeneric transport error. Validation enforces `required`, declared types and `enum` only —\n41\tit does not inject `additionalProperties: false`, coerce types, or apply defaults, and\n42\tthere is no opt-out flag.\n43\t\n44\tUpdate `docs/adding-a-tool.md`, `README.md` and `.specify/spec.md` to match.\n45\t\n46\t## Acceptance criteria\n47\t\n48\t- [ ] `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved definition's\n49\t      `inputSchema` before calling the executor; on failure it returns a `ToolResponse`\n50\t      whose `result` describes the failure, and the executor is never invoked\n51\t- [ ] A new `src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts` (the registry has no test file today)\n52\t      registers a tool requiring a `location` string, calls `execute` with `{}`, and\n53\t      asserts the returned result names `location` as required and the executor's call\n54\t      count is `0`\n55\t- [ ] A test asserts valid input with an unlisted extra property is passed through to the\n56\t      executor unchanged (`toEqual`, so an added default or coerced value fails the test) —\n57\t      exactly once\n58\t- [ ] A test asserts a wrong-typed field (a number where a string is required) is rejected\n59\t      with a message naming the field path and the expected type, and that the message does\n60\t      **not** contain the offending value\n61\t- [ ] A test asserts `register` throws at registration time for an uncompilable schema, and\n62\t      that the thrown message names the tool\n63\t- [ ] A test asserts a function-valued tool definition whose returned schema differs between\n64\t      calls is validated against the schema current at that call, not a stale cached one\n65\t- [ ] A test asserts a static tool's schema is compiled once across ten `execute` calls\n66\t- [ ] A tool-loop test in `chatOrchestrator.test.ts` drives a stub provider through a\n67\t      rejected tool call followed by a corrected retry, and asserts `processMessage`\n68\t      resolves — not rejects — with the rejection text visible to the provider on the next\n69\t      round\n70\t- [ ] `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name, unchanged,\n71\t      pinned by a test\n72\t- [ ] Rejection messages and log lines name the failing property paths and the constraint\n73\t      that failed, and never a property **value**. Tool input is where identifying data\n74\t      enters the engine and the engine redacts nothing today. A test asserts a rejected\n75\t      payload containing an identifying field produces no log line and no returned text\n76\t      containing that field's value\n77\t- [ ] `execute` carries a **one-line** doc comment — `re-lint/max-comment-lines` rejects a\n78\t      `@param`/`@returns`/`@throws` block — and the full contract (rejection `ToolResponse` for input\n79\t      the schema rejects, throw only for an unknown tool name) is stated in\n80\t      `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`, which the comment points at\n81\t- [ ] The validator is in `package.json` `dependencies`, not `peerDependencies` or\n82\t      `devDependencies`, and\n83\t      `npm run build && node --input-type=module -e \"import('./dist/index.js')\"` exits `0`\n84\t- [ ] `README.md` states what the registry now does, and the claim is true rather than\n85\t      merely present\n86\t- [ ] `docs/adding-a-tool.md` no longer tells executors to cast defensively and handle\n87\t      missing input; it states that input reaching an executor has already passed the\n88\t      declared schema, that unknown keys may still be present, and that a rejection is\n89\t      returned to the model rather than thrown\n90\t- [ ] `.specify/spec.md` records that a rejection is a tool result (not an exception), that\n91\t      the tool-round bound is what stops a retry cycle from spinning, and that extra\n92\t      properties pass through unmodified\n93\t- [ ] The PR body states the migration note: an executor that used to hand-validate its\n94\t      input now receives only input the schema already accepts, and a tool that relied on\n95\t      seeing malformed input to produce its own error message stops seeing it. The version\n96\t      bump is MINOR\n97\t- [ ] Every test this issue adds cites the statement it validates with an inline\n98\t      `([validated by ...])` link in a `specs/**` or `adrs/**` file, and the statements exist\n99\t      before the tests do. `re-lint/require-spec-link` fails the build on an uncited test, so the\n100\t      spec edits are part of this issue rather than a follow-up\n101\t- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry for this change, written for somebody reading it on npm\n102\t      rather than somebody reading this repo's log. CI runs `scripts/check-changelog.sh` and\n103\t      fails any PR touching a non-test file under `src/` without one\n104\t- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`)\n105\t      and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the changed public surface\n106\t\n107\t## Tech notes\n108\t\n109\tToday's `throw` in `registry.ts` propagates through the orchestrator's tool-call\n110\t`Promise.all`, out of the processing loop's `try`, fires `onError`, and reaches the\n111\tWebSocket error handler, which sends a generic \"Failed to process message\" and never sends\n112\tthe stream-end frame. That end-to-end path is why a rejection has to be a returned\n113\t`ToolResponse` instead of a throw.\n114\t\n115\tDeliberately out of scope: the `Unknown tool: <name>` throw stays as-is — the same failure\n116\tclass handled the opposite way, which is an intentional split, but changing it affects\n117\tdirect callers and is tracked as *Return an unknown tool name to the model instead of\n118\tthrowing*. Also out of scope: validating tool *output*; hallucination detection and an\n119\tevaluation harness; per-tool authorization — a schema-valid call for someone else's data is still\n120\tvalid, and authorization is a separate `ToolContext` boundary concern that no issue in this epic\n121\towns (*Let a consumer decline a tool call through a `beforeToolCall` hook* is a veto seam, not an\n122\tauthorization model); retry, timeout\n123\tand rate limiting around tool execution; and any actual tool implementation, which is\n124\twritten against this contract later.\n125\t\n126\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"085eaee9-e9f7-4fcf-ace0-bd2967c4b194","uuid":"5e4fffa8-98ab-447e-a033-9783d2f56111","timestamp":"2026-11-07T00:45:35.176Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/953780819e2e/workspace/ticket.md","content":"# Validate tool input against the registered inputSchema\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#37\nLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:M, area:tools, epic:extension-points\n\nPart of #35\n\n## Why\n\n`ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\nwithout ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\nEvery tool declares a JSON Schema (`src/types/ai.ts`) that is handed verbatim to both\nproviders, but nothing validates what comes back from the model. The shipped example shows the\nshape of the problem: `example/weather-tool.ts` declares `location` as a required string, then\ncasts and defaults it (`(input.location as string) || 'Unknown'`) with a comment warning the reader\nthat `inputSchema` shapes what the model sends but does not enforce it. That is the careful version,\nwritten by the people who wrote the engine — and it still answers a malformed call with a\nconfidently formatted forecast for `'Unknown'` rather than telling the model it got the call wrong.\n\nThe registry currently pushes this check onto each tool's own executor, leaving every consumer\nto implement it itself — differently each time, or not at all. A tool that receives unexpected input must fail\nin one predictable, known way before the rest of this epic's tool work can be built on top\nof it.\n\n## What to do\n\nValidate `input` against the resolved tool definition's `inputSchema` inside\n`ToolRegistry.execute`, before the executor runs, using a JSON Schema validator (`ajv`\nunless the build proves otherwise) added to `dependencies` — not a second, hand-written\nschema in a different format, since the schema already has to match what is handed to the\nproviders.\n\nCompile a static tool's schema once at `register` time so a broken schema fails at startup,\nnot mid-conversation; a schema produced by a function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource`\nresolves and compiles per call, cached by the resolved schema object.\n\nA rejection returns a `ToolResponse` (`{result: '<what was wrong>'}`) rather than throwing,\nso a malformed call becomes something the model can read and correct within the existing\n`DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5` budget, instead of ending the whole conversation with a\ngeneric transport error. Validation enforces `required`, declared types and `enum` only —\nit does not inject `additionalProperties: false`, coerce types, or apply defaults, and\nthere is no opt-out flag.\n\nUpdate `docs/adding-a-tool.md`, `README.md` and `.specify/spec.md` to match.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved definition's\n      `inputSchema` before calling the executor; on failure it returns a `ToolResponse`\n      whose `result` describes the failure, and the executor is never invoked\n- [ ] A new `src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts` (the registry has no test file today)\n      registers a tool requiring a `location` string, calls `execute` with `{}`, and\n      asserts the returned result names `location` as required and the executor's call\n      count is `0`\n- [ ] A test asserts valid input with an unlisted extra property is passed through to the\n      executor unchanged (`toEqual`, so an added default or coerced value fails the test) —\n      exactly once\n- [ ] A test asserts a wrong-typed field (a number where a string is required) is rejected\n      with a message naming the field path and the expected type, and that the message does\n      **not** contain the offending value\n- [ ] A test asserts `register` throws at registration time for an uncompilable schema, and\n      that the thrown message names the tool\n- [ ] A test asserts a function-valued tool definition whose returned schema differs between\n      calls is validated against the schema current at that call, not a stale cached one\n- [ ] A test asserts a static tool's schema is compiled once across ten `execute` calls\n- [ ] A tool-loop test in `chatOrchestrator.test.ts` drives a stub provider through a\n      rejected tool call followed by a corrected retry, and asserts `processMessage`\n      resolves — not rejects — with the rejection text visible to the provider on the next\n      round\n- [ ] `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name, unchanged,\n      pinned by a test\n- [ ] Rejection messages and log lines name the failing property paths and the constraint\n      that failed, and never a property **value**. Tool input is where identifying data\n      enters the engine and the engine redacts nothing today. A test asserts a rejected\n      payload containing an identifying field produces no log line and no returned text\n      containing that field's value\n- [ ] `execute` carries a **one-line** doc comment — `re-lint/max-comment-lines` rejects a\n      `@param`/`@returns`/`@throws` block — and the full contract (rejection `ToolResponse` for input\n      the schema rejects, throw only for an unknown tool name) is stated in\n      `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`, which the comment points at\n- [ ] The validator is in `package.json` `dependencies`, not `peerDependencies` or\n      `devDependencies`, and\n      `npm run build && node --input-type=module -e \"import('./dist/index.js')\"` exits `0`\n- [ ] `README.md` states what the registry now does, and the claim is true rather than\n      merely present\n- [ ] `docs/adding-a-tool.md` no longer tells executors to cast defensively and handle\n      missing input; it states that input reaching an executor has already passed the\n      declared schema, that unknown keys may still be present, and that a rejection is\n      returned to the model rather than thrown\n- [ ] `.specify/spec.md` records that a rejection is a tool result (not an exception), that\n      the tool-round bound is what stops a retry cycle from spinning, and that extra\n      properties pass through unmodified\n- [ ] The PR body states the migration note: an executor that used to hand-validate its\n      input now receives only input the schema already accepts, and a tool that relied on\n      seeing malformed input to produce its own error message stops seeing it. The version\n      bump is MINOR\n- [ ] Every test this issue adds cites the statement it validates with an inline\n      `([validated by ...])` link in a `specs/**` or `adrs/**` file, and the statements exist\n      before the tests do. `re-lint/require-spec-link` fails the build on an uncited test, so the\n      spec edits are part of this issue rather than a follow-up\n- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry for this change, written for somebody reading it on npm\n      rather than somebody reading this repo's log. CI runs `scripts/check-changelog.sh` and\n      fails any PR touching a non-test file under `src/` without one\n- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`)\n      and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the changed public surface\n\n## Tech notes\n\nToday's `throw` in `registry.ts` propagates through the orchestrator's tool-call\n`Promise.all`, out of the processing loop's `try`, fires `onError`, and reaches the\nWebSocket error handler, which sends a generic \"Failed to process message\" and never sends\nthe stream-end frame. That end-to-end path is why a rejection has to be a returned\n`ToolResponse` instead of a throw.\n\nDeliberately out of scope: the `Unknown tool: <name>` throw stays as-is — the same failure\nclass handled the opposite way, which is an intentional split, but changing it affects\ndirect callers and is tracked as *Return an unknown tool name to the model instead of\nthrowing*. Also out of scope: validating tool *output*; hallucination detection and an\nevaluation harness; per-tool authorization — a schema-valid call for someone else's data is still\nvalid, and authorization is a separate `ToolContext` boundary concern that no issue in this epic\nowns (*Let a consumer decline a tool call through a `beforeToolCall` hook* is a veto seam, not an\nauthorization model); retry, timeout\nand rate limiting around tool execution; and any actual tool implementation, which is\nwritten against this contract later.\n\n","numLines":126,"startLine":1,"totalLines":126}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01CdQGqwuGdpaFJBBFZL4hi9","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_013qEJ9Ww24ZepTiwAfud1vp","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Definition of Done\n2\t\n3\t> `ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\n4\t> without ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\n5\t> Every tool declares a JSON Schema (`src/types/ai.ts`) that is handed verbatim to both\n6\t> providers, but nothing validates what comes back from the model.\n7\t\n8\t**Strategy: `direct`** — `ToolRegistry.register`, `ToolRegistry.execute` and `createChatOrchestrator` already expose the exact entry points this ticket changes, so the acceptance tests call them directly today and fail because schema compilation and input validation are absent.\n9\t\n10\t## Done when these pass\n11\t\n12\t- [ ] **rejects input missing a required property with a ToolResponse and never calls the executor** — `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against `inputSchema`, returns a `ToolResponse` naming the missing required property, and does not invoke the executor.\n13\t  `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n14\t\n15\t- [ ] **rejects a wrong-typed property naming the field and expected type without leaking the offending value to the result or logs** — `ToolRegistry.execute` rejects a property with the wrong JSON Schema type without calling the executor, names the failing field path and expected type in `result`, and omits the offending property value from both `result` and emitted log lines.\n16\t  `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n17\t\n18\t- [ ] **throws at register time naming the tool when a static inputSchema cannot be compiled** — `ToolRegistry.register` compiles a static `ToolDefinition`'s `inputSchema` once at registration time and throws synchronously naming the tool when the schema is invalid.\n19\t  `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n20\t\n21\t- [ ] **returns a schema rejection as a tool_result in the orchestrator loop so the model can correct the call on the next round** — `createChatOrchestrator` feeds a schema-rejected tool call's `ToolResponse.result` back to the provider as a `tool_result` without invoking the executor on the invalid call, allowing the model to retry with valid arguments and complete the turn.\n22\t  `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n23\t\n24\t## Facets\n25\t\n26\t- [x] Add `ajv` to `dependencies` in `package.json` and `package-lock.json` (`strict: false`, `validateFormats: false`, no coercion, no defaults, no injected `additionalProperties: false`) and bump the package version `MINOR`.\n27\t- [x] Compile static `ToolDefinition.inputSchema` at `ToolRegistry.register` time (throwing an error naming the tool on invalid schemas) and cache compiled validators for function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource` schemas by schema object reference (`WeakMap`).\n28\t- [x] Validate `input` in `ToolRegistry.execute` before invoking the executor, returning a `ToolResponse` (`{result}`) that names failing field paths and expected types/required fields without echoing or logging offending values, and add the one-line doc comment pointing at `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`.\n29\t- [ ] Add unit tests in `src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts` and `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts`, link the remaining statements in `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`, and update `example/weather-tool.ts`, `docs/adding-a-tool.md`, `README.md`, `.specify/spec.md`, `adrs/ADR-002-response-validation.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md`.\n30\t\n31\t## Out of scope\n32\t\n33\t- Output validation and domain sanity checks (covered by ADR-002), MCP tool-server discovery and proxying (#42), per-tool authorization or `beforeToolCall` hooks (#40), retry/timeout/rate-limiting around tool execution, and concrete tool implementations.\n34\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"085eaee9-e9f7-4fcf-ace0-bd2967c4b194","uuid":"177ebe8d-5e5b-477f-b160-8fbeb17bb365","timestamp":"2026-11-07T00:45:35.596Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/953780819e2e/workspace/target/.lore/dod.md","content":"# Definition of Done\n\n> `ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\n> without ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\n> Every tool declares a JSON Schema (`src/types/ai.ts`) that is handed verbatim to both\n> providers, but nothing validates what comes back from the model.\n\n**Strategy: `direct`** — `ToolRegistry.register`, `ToolRegistry.execute` and `createChatOrchestrator` already expose the exact entry points this ticket changes, so the acceptance tests call them directly today and fail because schema compilation and input validation are absent.\n\n## Done when these pass\n\n- [ ] **rejects input missing a required property with a ToolResponse and never calls the executor** — `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against `inputSchema`, returns a `ToolResponse` naming the missing required property, and does not invoke the executor.\n  `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **rejects a wrong-typed property naming the field and expected type without leaking the offending value to the result or logs** — `ToolRegistry.execute` rejects a property with the wrong JSON Schema type without calling the executor, names the failing field path and expected type in `result`, and omits the offending property value from both `result` and emitted log lines.\n  `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **throws at register time naming the tool when a static inputSchema cannot be compiled** — `ToolRegistry.register` compiles a static `ToolDefinition`'s `inputSchema` once at registration time and throws synchronously naming the tool when the schema is invalid.\n  `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **returns a schema rejection as a tool_result in the orchestrator loop so the model can correct the call on the next round** — `createChatOrchestrator` feeds a schema-rejected tool call's `ToolResponse.result` back to the provider as a `tool_result` without invoking the executor on the invalid call, allowing the model to retry with valid arguments and complete the turn.\n  `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n## Facets\n\n- [x] Add `ajv` to `dependencies` in `package.json` and `package-lock.json` (`strict: false`, `validateFormats: false`, no coercion, no defaults, no injected `additionalProperties: false`) and bump the package version `MINOR`.\n- [x] Compile static `ToolDefinition.inputSchema` at `ToolRegistry.register` time (throwing an error naming the tool on invalid schemas) and cache compiled validators for function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource` schemas by schema object reference (`WeakMap`).\n- [x] Validate `input` in `ToolRegistry.execute` before invoking the executor, returning a `ToolResponse` (`{result}`) that names failing field paths and expected types/required fields without echoing or logging offending values, and add the one-line doc comment pointing at `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`.\n- [ ] Add unit tests in `src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts` and `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts`, link the remaining statements in `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`, and update `example/weather-tool.ts`, `docs/adding-a-tool.md`, `README.md`, `.specify/spec.md`, `adrs/ADR-002-response-validation.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md`.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- Output validation and domain sanity checks (covered by ADR-002), MCP tool-server discovery and proxying (#42), per-tool authorization or `beforeToolCall` hooks (#40), retry/timeout/rate-limiting around tool execution, and concrete tool implementations.\n","numLines":34,"startLine":1,"totalLines":34}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_013qEJ9Ww24ZepTiwAfud1vp","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{ToolRegistry} from './registry.js';\n\ndescribe('ToolRegistry', () => {\n  it('validates function-valued source against schema current at each call', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    let schema: Record<string, unknown> = {\n      type: 'object',\n      properties: {x: {type: 'string'}},\n      required: ['x'],\n    };\n    registry.register(\n      () => ({name: 'flex', description: 'flexible', inputSchema: schema}),\n      async () => 'ok'\n    );\n\n    const r1 = await registry.execute('flex', {x: 'hello'});\n    schema = {type: 'object', {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Xm5fiFLn6aigLxom6rBgZh","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport Ajv from 'ajv';\n2\timport type {ToolDefinition} from '../../types/ai.js';\n3\timport type {OutgoingMessage} from '../../types/messages.js';\n4\timport {log} from '../../shared/logger.js';\n5\t\n6\tconst ajv = new Ajv({allErrors: true});\n7\t\n8\texport interface ToolResponse {\n9\t  result: string;\n10\t  clientMessages?: OutgoingMessage[];\n11\t  suppressAssistantResponse?: boolean;\n12\t}\n13\t\n14\texport interface ToolContext {\n15\t  userId: string | number;\n16\t  sessionId?: string;\n17\t  workspaceId?: string | number;\n18\t  authHeaders?: {\n19\t    cookie?: string;\n20\t    authorization?: string;\n21\t    host?: string;\n22\t  };\n23\t}\n24\t\n25\texport type ToolExecutor = (input: Record<string, unknown>, context?: ToolContext) => Promise<string | ToolResponse>;\n26\t\n27\texport function normalizeToolResponse(response: string | ToolResponse): ToolResponse {\n28\t  return typeof response === 'string' ? {result: response} : response;\n29\t}\n30\t\n31\texport type ToolDefinitionSource = ToolDefinition | (() => ToolDefinition);\n32\t\n33\texport interface RegisteredTool {\n34\t  definition: ToolDefinition;\n35\t  execute: ToolExecutor;\n36\t}\n37\t\n38\ttype ValidateFunction = ReturnType<typeof ajv.compile>;\n39\t\n40\tconst functionSourceValidators = new WeakMap<Record<string, unknown>, ValidateFunction>();\n41\t\n42\tfunction compileSchema(schema: Record<string, unknown>, toolName: string): ValidateFunction {\n43\t  try {\n44\t    return ajv.compile(schema as object);\n45\t  } catch (err) {\n46\t    throw new Error(`Tool \"${toolName}\": invalid inputSchema — ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}`);\n47\t  }\n48\t}\n49\t\n50\tfunction getValidator(schema: Record<string, unknown>, toolName: string): ValidateFunction {\n51\t  let validate = functionSourceValidators.get(schema);\n52\t  if (!validate) {\n53\t    validate = compileSchema(schema, toolName);\n54\t    functionSourceValidators.set(schema, validate);\n55\t  }\n56\t  return validate;\n57\t}\n58\t\n59\tinterface StoredTool {\n60\t  definitionSource: ToolDefinitionSource;\n61\t  execute: ToolExecutor;\n62\t  staticValidator?: ValidateFunction;\n63\t}\n64\t\n65\texport class ToolRegistry {\n66\t  private readonly tools = new Map<string, StoredTool>();\n67\t\n68\t  private resolveDefinition(source: ToolDefinitionSource): ToolDefinition {\n69\t    return typeof source === 'function' ? source() : source;\n70\t  }\n71\t\n72\t  register(definition: ToolDefinitionSource, execute: ToolExecutor): void {\n73\t    const resolved = this.resolveDefinition(definition);\n74\t    let staticValidator: ValidateFunction | undefined;\n75\t    if (typeof definition !== 'function') {\n76\t      staticValidator = compileSchema(resolved.inputSchema as Record<string, unknown>, resolved.name);\n77\t    }\n78\t    this.tools.set(resolved.name, {definitionSource: definition, execute, staticValidator});\n79\t  }\n80\t\n81\t  get(name: string): RegisteredTool | undefined {\n82\t    const tool = this.tools.get(name);\n83\t    if (!tool) return undefined;\n84\t    return {definition: this.resolveDefinition(tool.definitionSource), execute: tool.execute};\n85\t  }\n86\t\n87\t  getDefinitions(): ToolDefinition[] {\n88\t    return [...this.tools.values()].map(t => this.resolveDefinition(t.definitionSource));\n89\t  }\n90\t\n91\t  getPromptInstructions(): string[] {\n92\t    return this.getDefinitions()\n93\t      .filter(t => t.promptInstructions)\n94\t      .map(t => `${t.name}: ${t.promptInstructions}`);\n95\t  }\n96\t\n97\t  getExamplePrompts(): string[] {\n98\t    return this.getDefinitions().flatMap(t => t.examplePrompts ?? []);\n99\t  }\n100\t\n101\t  has(name: string): boolean {\n102\t    return this.tools.has(name);\n103\t  }\n104\t\n105\t  // Validates input against the tool's declared inputSchema; see specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md.\n106\t  async execute(name: string, input: Record<string, unknown>, context?: ToolContext): Promise<ToolResponse> {\n107\t    const tool = this.tools.get(name);\n108\t    if (!tool) {\n109\t      log.warn('tool', 'tool not found', {name});\n110\t      throw new Error(`Unknown tool: ${name}`);\n111\t    }\n112\t    const definition = this.resolveDefinition(tool.definitionSource);\n113\t    const validate =\n114\t      tool.staticValidator ?? getValidator(definition.inputSchema as Record<string, unknown>, definition.name);\n115\t    if (!validate(input)) {\n116\t      log.warn('tool', 'input validation failed', {name, errorCount: validate.errors?.length ?? 0});\n117\t      return {result: ajv.errorsText(validate.errors)};\n118\t    }\n119\t    log.info('tool', 'executing', {name, inputKeys: Object.keys(input)});\n120\t    const rawResult = await tool.execute(input, context);\n121\t    const response = normalizeToolResponse(rawResult);\n122\t    log.info('tool', 'execution complete', {\n123\t      name,\n124\t      rawType: typeof rawResult,\n125\t      clientMessageCount: response.clientMessages?.length ?? 0,\n126\t      clientMessageTypes: response.clientMessages?.map(m => m.type) ?? {result: response} : response;\n}\n\nexport type ToolDefinitionSource = ToolDefinition | (() => ToolDefinition);\n\nexport interface RegisteredTool {\n  definition: ToolDefinition;\n  execute: ToolExecutor;\n}\n\ntype ValidateFunction = ReturnType<typeof ajv.compile>;\n\nconst functionSourceValidators = new WeakMap<Record<string, unknown>, ValidateFunction>();\n\nfunction compileSchema(schema: Record<string, unknown>, toolName: string): ValidateFunction {\n  try {\n    return ajv.compile(schema as object);\n  } catch (err) {\n    throw new Error(`Tool \"${toolName}\": invalid inputSchema — ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}`);\n  }\n}\n\nfunction getValidator(schema: Record<string, unknown>, toolName: string): ValidateFunction {\n  let validate = functionSourceValidators.get(schema);\n  if (!validate) {\n    validate = compileSchema(schema, toolName);\n    functionSourceValidators.set(schema, validate);\n  }\n  return validate;\n}\n\ninterface StoredTool {\n  definitionSource: ToolDefinitionSource;\n  execute: ToolExecutor;\n  staticValidator?: ValidateFunction;\n}\n\nexport class ToolRegistry {\n  private readonly tools = new Map<string, StoredTool>();\n\n  private resolveDefinition(source: ToolDefinitionSource): ToolDefinition {\n    return typeof source === 'function' ? source() : source;\n  }\n\n  register(definition: ToolDefinitionSource, execute: ToolExecutor): void {\n    const resolved = this.resolveDefinition(definition);\n    let staticValidator: ValidateFunction | undefined;\n    if (typeof definition !== 'function') {\n      staticValidator = compileSchema(resolved.inputSchema as Record<string, unknown>, resolved.name);\n    }\n    this.tools.set(resolved.name, {definitionSource: definition, execute, staticValidator});\n  }\n\n  get(name: string): RegisteredTool | undefined {\n    const tool = this.tools.get(name);\n    if (!tool) return undefined;\n    return {definition: this.resolveDefinition(tool.definitionSource), execute: tool.execute};\n  }\n\n  getDefinitions(): ToolDefinition[] {\n    return [...this.tools.values()].map(t => this.resolveDefinition(t.definitionSource));\n  }\n\n  getPromptInstructions(): string[] {\n    return this.getDefinitions()\n      .filter(t => t.promptInstructions)\n      .map(t => `${t.name}: ${t.promptInstructions}`);\n  }\n\n  getExamplePrompts(): string[] {\n    return this.getDefinitions().flatMap(t => t.examplePrompts ?? []);\n  }\n\n  has(name: string): boolean {\n    return this.tools.has(name);\n  }\n\n  // Validates input against the tool's declared inputSchema; see specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md.\n  async execute(name: string, input: Record<string, unknown>, context?: ToolContext): Promise<ToolResponse> {\n    const tool = this.tools.get(name);\n    if (!tool) {\n      log.warn('tool', 'tool not found', {name});\n      throw new Error(`Unknown tool: ${name}`);\n    }\n    const definition = this.resolveDefinition(tool.definitionSource);\n    const validate =\n      tool.staticValidator ?? getValidator(definition.inputSchema as Record<string, unknown>, definition.name);\n    if (!validate(input)) {\n      log.warn('tool', 'input validation failed', {name, errorCount: validate.errors?.length ?? 0});\n      return {result: ajv.errorsText(validate.errors)};\n    }\n    log.info('tool', 'executing', {name, inputKeys: Object.keys(input)});\n    const rawResult = await tool.execute(input, context);\n    const response = normalizeToolResponse(rawResult);\n    log.info('tool', 'execution complete', {\n      name,\n      rawType: typeof rawResult,\n      clientMessageCount: response.clientMessages?.length ?? 0,\n      clientMessageTypes: response.clientMessages?.map(m => m.type) ?? [],\n      suppressAssistantResponse: response.suppressAssistantResponse ?? false,\n    });\n    return response;\n  }\n}\n","numLines":132,"startLine":1,"totalLines":132}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01Xm5fiFLn6aigLxom6rBgZh","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-6","id":"msg_011CgneJTwn8jKWXfiZNx5hm","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01K88ar3KeF1kA5ZWsnTC5Ms","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"ls /var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/953780819e2e/workspace/target/specs/ 2>/dev/null && cat /var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/953780819e2e/workspace/target/specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md 2>/dev/null || echo \"SPEC NOT FOUND\"","description":"Check spec file for tool input validation"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3018,"cache_read_input_tokens":30469,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":3018},"output_tokens":8,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global","speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"085eaee9-e9f7-4fcf-ace0-bd2967c4b194","uuid":"b12175c2-4426-4c76-a252-66dbb422d2db","timestamp":"2026-11-07T00:45:50.705Z","request_id":"req_011CgneJTmd44vEf5oe8XQMi"}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01K88ar3KeF1kA5ZWsnTC5Ms","type":"tool_result","content":"hal-engine-architecture\nhal-engine-chat-routes\nhal-engine-config-hooks\nhal-engine-doc-gates\nhal-engine-npm-release\nhal-engine-providers\nhal-engine-spec-anchor-drift\nhal-engine-spec-link-placement\nhal-engine-spec-status\nhal-engine-thinking-tag-parser\nhal-engine-tool-input-validation\nhal-engine-tool-responses\nhal-engine-websocket-protocol\n# Tool Input Validation\n\n| Field  | Value                |\n| ------ | -------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#37 |\n| Status | In Progress          |\n\nEvery tool registered in `src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts` declares a JSON Schema `inputSchema` that is forwarded to the configured AI provider, and `ToolRegistry.execute` validates the model-supplied `input` against that schema before calling the tool's executor. When validation fails, `execute` returns a rejection `ToolResponse` describing what was wrong rather than throwing, so the orchestrator feeds the failure back to the model as a `tool_result` within the existing tool-round budget.\n\n## Validation at execute time\n\n- When `input` omits a property listed in `required`, `ToolRegistry.execute` returns a `ToolResponse` whose `result` names the missing property as required and never invokes the executor ([validated by: rejects input missing a required property with a ToolResponse and never calls the executor](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L16)).\n- When `input` supplies a property whose value does not match its declared JSON Schema type, `ToolRegistry.execute` returns a `ToolResponse` naming the failing field path and expected type without invoking the executor, and neither `result` nor the emitted log record contains the offending property value ([validated by: rejects a wrong-typed property naming the field and expected type without leaking the offending value to the result or logs](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L46)).\n- When `input` satisfies `required`, declared property types and `enum`, `ToolRegistry.execute` invokes the executor once and passes the input object through unchanged — including undeclared extra properties — without injecting `additionalProperties: false`, coercing types or applying defaults.\n- When `ToolRegistry.execute` is called with a tool name that has not been registered, it throws `Unknown tool: <name>` because an unregistered tool is a programming or configuration error rather than a recoverable input mistake.\n\n## Schema compilation and caching\n\n- A static `ToolDefinition` has its `inputSchema` compiled once at `register` time, so a schema that is not valid JSON Schema throws synchronously from `register` with an error message naming the offending tool ([validated by: throws at register time naming the tool when a static inputSchema cannot be compiled](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L94)).\n- A static tool's `inputSchema` is compiled at `register` time and reused on every `execute` call without re-reading or re-compiling `definition.inputSchema`.\n- A function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource` resolves its definition on each `execute` call and validates against the `inputSchema` returned for that call, caching compiled validators by schema object reference (`WeakMap`) so distinct returned schemas each enforce their own rules ([validated by: validates function-valued source against schema current at each call](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts#L4)).\n\n## Orchestrator tool-loop recovery\n\n- Because a schema rejection returns a `ToolResponse` instead of throwing, `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` sends the rejection `result` back to the provider as a `tool_result` message, allowing the model to issue a corrected tool call on the next round and complete the turn within `maxToolRounds` ([validated by: returns a schema rejection as a tool_result in the orchestrator loop so the model can correct the call on the next round](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L112)).","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"085eaee9-e9f7-4fcf-ace0-bd2967c4b194","uuid":"32caa211-1aea-4073-850c-9fbae93d2565","timestamp":"2026-11-07T00:45:50.757Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"hal-engine-architecture\nhal-engine-chat-routes\nhal-engine-config-hooks\nhal-engine-doc-gates\nhal-engine-npm-release\nhal-engine-providers\nhal-engine-spec-anchor-drift\nhal-engine-spec-link-placement\nhal-engine-spec-status\nhal-engine-thinking-tag-parser\nhal-engine-tool-input-validation\nhal-engine-tool-responses\nhal-engine-websocket-protocol\n# Tool Input Validation\n\n| Field  | Value                |\n| ------ | -------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#37 |\n| Status | In Progress          |\n\nEvery tool registered in `src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts` declares a JSON Schema `inputSchema` that is forwarded to the configured AI provider, and `ToolRegistry.execute` validates the model-supplied `input` against that schema before calling the tool's executor. When validation fails, `execute` returns a rejection `ToolResponse` describing what was wrong rather than throwing, so the orchestrator feeds the failure back to the model as a `tool_result` within the existing tool-round budget.\n\n## Validation at execute time\n\n- When `input` omits a property listed in `required`, `ToolRegistry.execute` returns a `ToolResponse` whose `result` names the missing property as required and never invokes the executor ([validated by: rejects input missing a required property with a ToolResponse and never calls the executor](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L16)).\n- When `input` supplies a property whose value does not match its declared JSON Schema type, `ToolRegistry.execute` returns a `ToolResponse` naming the failing field path and expected type without invoking the executor, and neither `result` nor the emitted log record contains the offending property value ([validated by: rejects a wrong-typed property naming the field and expected type without leaking the offending value to the result or logs](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L46)).\n- When `input` satisfies `required`, declared property types and `enum`, `ToolRegistry.execute` invokes the executor once and passes the input object through unchanged — including undeclared extra properties — without injecting `additionalProperties: false`, coercing types or applying defaults.\n- When `ToolRegistry.execute` is called with a tool name that has not been registered, it throws `Unknown tool: <name>` because an unregistered tool is a programming or configuration error rather than a recoverable input mistake.\n\n## Schema compilation and caching\n\n- A static `ToolDefinition` has its `inputSchema` compiled once at `register` time, so a schema that is not valid JSON Schema throws synchronously from `register` with an error message naming the offending tool ([validated by: throws at register time naming the tool when a static inputSchema cannot be compiled](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L94)).\n- A static tool's `inputSchema` is compiled at `register` time and reused on every `execute` call without re-reading or re-compiling `definition.inputSchema`.\n- A function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource` resolves its definition on each `execute` call and validates against the `inputSchema` returned for that call, caching compiled validators by schema object reference (`WeakMap`) so distinct returned schemas each enforce their own rules ([validated by: validates function-valued source against schema current at each call](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts#L4)).\n\n## Orchestrator tool-loop recovery\n\n- Because a schema rejection returns a `ToolResponse` instead of throwing, `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` sends the rejection `result` back to the provider as a `tool_result` message, allowing the model to issue a corrected tool call on the next round and complete the turn within `maxToolRounds` ([validated by: returns a schema rejection as a tool_result in the orchestrator loop so the model can correct the call on the next round](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L112)).","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01K88ar3KeF1kA5ZWsnTC5Ms","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01J93gD6bMkEmiuPw1q1kAi1","type":"tool_result","content":"# Changelog\n\nAll notable changes to `@re-cinq/hal-engine` are documented here.\n\nThe format is [Keep a Changelog 1.1.0](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and this project adheres to\n[Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html). Entries are written for somebody installing the\npackage, not for somebody reading this repository's commit log.\n\n## [Unreleased]\n\n## [0.3.0] - 2026-09-17\n\n### Changed\n\n- `HalEngineConfig.orchestrator.hooks` now carries a JSDoc comment. The field already shipped\n  in `0.2.x`; the comment makes the lifecycle-hook seam discoverable from the published type\n  declarations so editors surface it on hover without a trip to the README.\n\n## [0.2.1] - 2026-09-14\n\nIdentical to `0.2.0` — same files, same code. It exists to prove that a release publishes with no\ncredential stored in the repository: `0.2.0` had to be published with a token, because the registry\ncannot hold a trusted publisher for a name that has never been published, and this is the first version\nreleased through that publisher instead. There is nothing here to upgrade for.\n\n## [0.2.0] - 2026-09-14\n\nThe first release under the `@re-cinq` scope. Nothing has been published before it, so there is no upgrade\npath from `0.1.0` on the registry — only from the git specifier.\n\n### Changed\n\n- The published package carries no source maps. `files` is `[\"dist\"]`, so every `.js.map` and `.d.ts.map`\n  named a `../src/*.ts` the tarball did not contain and carried no inlined sources — 98 files that resolved\n  to nothing in a debugger. Dropping them halves the file count. Step through the source from a checkout\n  of the repository instead.\n\n- The README documents the three environment variables this package actually reads — `CORS_ORIGIN`, `PORT`\n  and `LOG_LEVEL` — each with its reading site, what overrides it, and its default. `AWS_REGION` and\n  `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` are gone from the provider docs: both are config fields, and setting the variable\n  while passing a different `region` or `projectId` silently gives you the config value. Vendor credential\n  variables are still listed, now stating that this package reads none of them — the vendor SDK does.\n\n- The README now states which install path is supported. The registry specifier is the product; a git\n  specifier builds from a checkout, carries no provenance, and pins a commit rather than a version. If you\n  install from git under the key `hal-engine`, note that the rename is silent — `import 'hal-engine'` keeps\n  working and `import '@re-cinq/hal-engine'` is what fails, because npm installs under the dependency key\n  rather than the package name.\n\n- **Log output is JSON, one object per line.** Lines were unstructured text (`[time] [LEVEL] [category] msg`),","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"085eaee9-e9f7-4fcf-ace0-bd2967c4b194","uuid":"6056877a-502a-411f-af13-1272fb84f867","timestamp":"2026-11-07T00:46:11.484Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"# Changelog\n\nAll notable changes to `@re-cinq/hal-engine` are documented here.\n\nThe format is [Keep a Changelog 1.1.0](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and this project adheres to\n[Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html). Entries are written for somebody installing the\npackage, not for somebody reading this repository's commit log.\n\n## [Unreleased]\n\n## [0.3.0] - 2026-09-17\n\n### Changed\n\n- `HalEngineConfig.orchestrator.hooks` now carries a JSDoc comment. The field already shipped\n  in `0.2.x`; the comment makes the lifecycle-hook seam discoverable from the published type\n  declarations so editors surface it on hover without a trip to the README.\n\n## [0.2.1] - 2026-09-14\n\nIdentical to `0.2.0` — same files, same code. It exists to prove that a release publishes with no\ncredential stored in the repository: `0.2.0` had to be published with a token, because the registry\ncannot hold a trusted publisher for a name that has never been published, and this is the first version\nreleased through that publisher instead. There is nothing here to upgrade for.\n\n## [0.2.0] - 2026-09-14\n\nThe first release under the `@re-cinq` scope. Nothing has been published before it, so there is no upgrade\npath from `0.1.0` on the registry — only from the git specifier.\n\n### Changed\n\n- The published package carries no source maps. `files` is `[\"dist\"]`, so every `.js.map` and `.d.ts.map`\n  named a `../src/*.ts` the tarball did not contain and carried no inlined sources — 98 files that resolved\n  to nothing in a debugger. Dropping them halves the file count. Step through the source from a checkout\n  of the repository instead.\n\n- The README documents the three environment variables this package actually reads — `CORS_ORIGIN`, `PORT`\n  and `LOG_LEVEL` — each with its reading site, what overrides it, and its default. `AWS_REGION` and\n  `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` are gone from the provider docs: both are config fields, and setting the variable\n  while passing a different `region` or `projectId` silently gives you the config value. Vendor credential\n  variables are still listed, now stating that this package reads none of them — the vendor SDK does.\n\n- The README now states which install path is supported. The registry specifier is the product; a git\n  specifier builds from a checkout, carries no provenance, and pins a commit rather than a version. If you\n  install from git under the key `hal-engine`, note that the rename is silent — `import 'hal-engine'` keeps\n  working and `import '@re-cinq/hal-engine'` is what fails, because npm installs under the dependency key\n  rather than the package name.\n\n- **Log output is JSON, one object per line.** Lines were unstructured text (`[time] [LEVEL] [category] msg`),","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01J93gD6bMkEmiuPw1q1kAi1","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01P6wef1nfEsNsXJnCmkHXhN","type":"tool_result","content":"# Adding a New Tool\n\nTools let the AI model fetch data or perform actions during a conversation. When the model decides it needs information (like weather data or a database lookup), it invokes a tool by name, the server executes it, and the result feeds back into the conversation.\n\nAdding a new tool takes two steps: define it and register it. The rest is automatic -- the orchestrator passes tools to the AI provider, and the client already knows how to render any tool entry.\n\n## Step 1: Create the tool file\n\nCreate a new file in `src/orchestration/tools/`. Each tool exports two things:\n\n- A **`ToolDefinition`** describing the tool's name, purpose, and input schema\n- A **`ToolExecutor`** function that runs the tool and returns a JSON string\n\nHere is a complete example:\n\n<!-- doc-block: example/weather-tool.ts#weather-tool -->\n```typescript\nimport type {ToolDefinition, ToolExecutor} from '@re-cinq/hal-engine';\n\nexport const weatherTool: ToolDefinition = {\n  name: 'get_weather',\n  description:\n    'Get the current weather for a location.\\n\\n' +\n    'Use this tool when the user:\\n' +\n    '* Asks about weather or temperature\\n' +\n    '* Wants to know conditions at a specific place\\n' +\n    '* Needs a forecast for planning\\n\\n' +\n    'Returns temperature, conditions, humidity, and wind speed.',\n  promptInstructions:\n    'Use this when users ask about weather at a specific location. ' +\n    'Call this for questions like \"What is the weather in Berlin?\" or \"Is it raining in Tokyo?\"',\n  examplePrompts: ['What is the weather in Berlin?', 'Is it raining in Tokyo?'],\n  inputSchema: {\n    type: 'object',\n    properties: {\n      location: {\n        type: 'string',\n        description: 'The city name or location to look up.',\n      },\n      units: {\n        type: 'string',\n        enum: ['celsius', 'fahrenheit'],\n        description: 'Temperature units. Defaults to celsius.',\n      },\n    },\n    required: ['location'],\n  },\n};\n\nexport const executeWeather: ToolExecutor = async input => {\n  const location = (input.location as string) || 'Unknown';\n  const units = (input.units as string) || 'celsius';\n\n  // Replace with a real API call. `input` is untrusted: inputSchema shapes what the model sends, it does not enforce it.\n  const result = {\n    location,\n    temperature: 18,\n    units,\n    conditions: 'Partly cloudy',\n    humidity: 65,\n    windSpeed: 12,\n    windUnit: 'km/h',\n  };\n\n  return JSON.stringify(result);\n};\n```\n\n### Writing a good tool description\n\nThe `description` field is what the AI model reads to decide when to use the tool. Be specific:\n\n- List the situations where the tool should be used (\"Use this tool when...\")\n- Describe what the tool returns\n- Give example queries if it helps\n\nThe more precise the description, the better the model's judgment about when to invoke it.\n\n### Adding prompt instructions\n\nThe optional `promptInstructions` field provides guidance that gets included in the AI's system prompt. Unlike `description` (which is part of the tool schema sent to the AI provider), `promptInstructions` appears in the system prompt text itself, giving higher-level behavioral rules about when and how to use the tool.\n\n<!-- doc-block: none -- one field of a ToolDefinition, quoted to discuss how to word it -->\n```typescript\npromptInstructions:\n  'Use this when users ask about weather at a specific location. ' +\n  'Call this for questions like \"What is the weather in Berlin?\" or \"Is it raining in Tokyo?\"',\n```\n\nThe `ToolRegistry.getPromptInstructions()` method collects these from all registered tools, and the `PromptBuilder` assembles them into the system prompt automatically. If you don't provide `promptInstructions`, the tool still works -- it just won't have explicit guidance in the system prompt.\n\nUse `promptInstructions` for:\n- Short, actionable rules about when to call the tool\n- Critical behavioral constraints (e.g., \"MUST call this for every weather question\")\n- Example queries that help the model match user intent\n\nDon't duplicate what's already in `description` -- keep `promptInstructions` focused on behavioral rules rather than describing inputs/outputs.\n\n### Adding example prompts\n\nThe optional `examplePrompts` field provides example queries that are shown to users in the chat interface as clickable suggestions. These are sent to the client in the WebSocket `connected` message and persist throughout the conversation.\n\n<!-- doc-block: none -- one field of a ToolDefinition, quoted to discuss how to word it -->\n```typescript\nexamplePrompts: ['What is the weather in Berlin?', 'Is it raining in Tokyo?'],\n```\n\nThe `ToolRegistry.getExamplePrompts()` method collects and flattens these from all registered tools. They are computed once at server startup and included in every new WebSocket connection's `connected` message.\n\nGuidelines:\n- Write prompts as natural user questions, not technical descriptions\n- Keep them short and specific -- they appear as pill-shaped buttons in the UI\n- 1-2 prompts per tool is enough; the total across all tools should stay manageable\n- Use realistic terminology relevant to your application domain\n\n### Defining the input schema\n\nThe `inputSchema` follows [JSON Schema](https://json-schema.org/) format. The model uses this to construct the correct input.\n\n- Use `required` to mark mandatory fields\n- Add `description` to each property -- the model reads these\n- Tool parameter names use `snake_case` (this is the convention from the AI provider API, wrap them in `/* eslint-disable camelcase */` comments)\n\n### Writing the executor\n\nA `ToolExecutor` is an async function that takes the tool input and returns either a string or a `ToolResponse`:\n\n<!-- doc-block: src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts#ToolExecutor -->\n```typescript\ntype ToolExecutor = (input: Record<string, unknown>, context?: ToolContext) => Promise<string | ToolResponse>;\n```\n\nThe optional `context` provides `userId`, `sessionId`, `workspaceId`, and `authHeaders` for tools that need to make authenticated API calls.\n\n- Return `JSON.stringify(result)` for most tools -- the result gets added to the conversation as a tool result message\n- Cast input fields from `unknown` to their expected types\n- Handle missing or unknown input gracefully (return a reasonable default or error message)\n\nFor tools that need to send messages directly to the client or suppress the AI's echo, return a `ToolResponse` object instead of a plain string. See [tool-responses.md](../specs/hal-engine-tool-responses/spec.md) for a full walkthrough.\n\n## Step 2: Register the tool\n\nOpen `src/orchestration/tools/index.ts` and add two lines:\n\n<!-- doc-block: example/register-tool.ts#register -->\n```typescript\nimport {weatherTool, executeWeather} from './weather-tool.js';\n\n// ... existing registrations ...\n\ntoolRegistry.register(weatherTool, executeWeather);\n```\n\nThat is it. The `ToolRegistry` feeds all registered tool definitions to the chat orchestrator, which passes them to the AI provider. When the model invokes the tool, the registry looks up the executor by name and runs it.\n\n## What happens automatically\n\nOnce a tool is registered, the following work without any additional code:\n\n**On the backend:**\n\n- `toolRegistry.getExamplePrompts()` collects example prompts from all tools and includes them in the WebSocket `connected` message\n- `chatOrchestrator.ts` calls `toolRegistry.getPromptInstructions()` to include tool guidance in the system prompt via `PromptBuilder`\n- `chatOrchestrator.ts` calls `toolRegistry.getDefinitions()` to pass all tools to the AI provider\n- When the provider returns a `tool_use` chunk, the message handler creates a `ToolEntry` and sends it via `entry_upsert`\n- The orchestrator executes all pending tool calls in parallel with `Promise.all()`\n- Tool results are added to the conversation, and the provider is re-queried (up to `maxToolRounds` rounds)\n\n**On the client:**\n\n- Tool entries can be rendered by the client using the `toolName` and `toolInput` fields\n- `formatToolName()` strips common prefixes (`get_`, `calculate_`, `fetch_`, `find_`) and title-cases the remaining words. So `get_weather` becomes **Weather**\n- `formatParamName()` converts `snake_case` parameter names to Title Case for display. So `wind_speed` becomes **Wind Speed**\n- A spinner shows on the last tool entry until a subsequent entry arrives\n\n## File reference\n\n| File | What it contains |\n|------|-----------------|\n| `src/types/ai.ts` | `ToolDefinition` interface (includes `promptInstructions`, `examplePrompts`) |\n| `src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts` | `ToolExecutor` type, `ToolResponse` interface, `ToolRegistry` class (includes `getPromptInstructions()`, `getExamplePrompts()`) |\n| `src/orchestration/tools/index.ts` | Tool registration (add your import and `register()` call here) |\n| `src/infrastructure/builders/promptBuilder.ts` | `PromptBuilder` class that assembles the system prompt from tool instructions |\n| `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` | Where tools are passed to the provider and executed |","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"085eaee9-e9f7-4fcf-ace0-bd2967c4b194","uuid":"d395db93-dfbc-41c0-854f-8ec0ec2ed03f","timestamp":"2026-11-07T00:46:14.248Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"# Adding a New Tool\n\nTools let the AI model fetch data or perform actions during a conversation. See [tool-responses.md](../specs/hal-engine-tool-responses/spec.md) for a full walkthrough.\n\n## Step 2: Register the tool\n\nOpen `src/orchestration/tools/index.ts` and add two lines:\n\n<!-- doc-block: example/register-tool.ts#register -->\n```typescript\nimport {weatherTool, executeWeather} from './weather-tool.js';\n\n// ... existing registrations ...\n\ntoolRegistry.register(weatherTool, executeWeather);\n```\n\nThat is it. The `ToolRegistry` feeds all registered tool definitions to the chat orchestrator, which passes them to the AI provider. When the model invokes the tool, the registry looks up the executor by name and runs it.\n\n## What happens automatically\n\nOnce a tool is registered, the following work without any additional code:\n\n**On the backend:**\n\n- `toolRegistry.getExamplePrompts()` collects example prompts from all tools and includes them in the WebSocket `connected` message\n- `chatOrchestrator.ts` calls `toolRegistry.getPromptInstructions()` to include tool guidance in the system prompt via `PromptBuilder`\n- `chatOrchestrator.ts` calls `toolRegistry.getDefinitions()` to pass all tools to the AI provider\n- When the provider returns a `tool_use` chunk, the message handler creates a `ToolEntry` and sends it via `entry_upsert`\n- The orchestrator executes all pending tool calls in parallel with `Promise.all()`\n- Tool results are added to the conversation, and the provider is re-queried (up to `maxToolRounds` rounds)\n\n**On the client:**\n\n- Tool entries can be rendered by the client using the `toolName` and `toolInput` fields\n- `formatToolName()` strips common prefixes (`get_`, `calculate_`, `fetch_`, `find_`) and title-cases the remaining words. So `get_weather` becomes **Weather**\n- `formatParamName()` converts `snake_case` parameter names to Title Case for display. So `wind_speed` becomes **Wind Speed**\n- A spinner shows on the last tool entry until a subsequent entry arrives\n\n## File reference\n\n| File | What it contains |\n|------|-----------------|\n| `src/types/ai.ts` | `ToolDefinition` interface (includes `promptInstructions`, `examplePrompts`) |\n| `src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts` | `ToolExecutor` type, `ToolResponse` interface, `ToolRegistry` class (includes `getPromptInstructions()`, `getExamplePrompts()`) |\n| `src/orchestration/tools/index.ts` | Tool registration (add your import and `register()` call here) |\n| `src/infrastructure/builders/promptBuilder.ts` | `PromptBuilder` class that assembles the system prompt from tool instructions |\n| `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` | Where tools are passed to the provider and executed |","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01P6wef1nfEsNsXJnCmkHXhN","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

''} --experimental-vm-modules`.trim()},\n  });\n} catch {\n  // A failing test still produces a full report, and listing must not depend\n  // on the suite being green. An unreadable report below is the real failure.\n}\n\nlet report;\n\ntry {\n  report = JSON.parse(readFileSync(reportFile, 'utf8'));\n} catch (error) {\n  process.stderr.write(`jest produced no readable report at ${reportFile}: ${error.message}\\n`);\n  process.exit(1);\n} finally {\n  rmSync(reportFile, {force: true});\n}\n\nconst tests = report.testResults.flatMap(suite => {\n  const file = relative(process.cwd(), suite.name);\n\n  return suite.assertionResults.map(assertion => ({\n    id: `${file}::${assertion.fullName}`,\n    name: assertion.fullName,\n    file,\n  }));\n});\n\nprocess.stdout.write(`${JSON.stringify(tests)}\\n`);","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"085eaee9-e9f7-4fcf-ace0-bd2967c4b194","uuid":"6f22f91c-8f4f-4da8-93ae-a4945d32d585","timestamp":"2026-11-07T00:47:12.688Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"check-action-pins.mjs\ncheck-action-pins.test.ts\ncheck-audit.mjs\ncheck-audit.test.ts\ncheck-changelog.sh\ncheck-changelog.test.ts\ncheck-doc-blocks.mjs\ncheck-doc-blocks.test.ts\ncheck-doc-gates.test.ts\ncheck-doc-paths.mjs\ncheck-doc-regions.mjs\ncheck-spec-anchor-names.mjs\ncheck-spec-anchor-names.test.ts\ncheck-spec-links.mjs\ncheck-spec-links.test.ts\ncheck-spec-status.mjs\ncheck-spec-status.test.ts\ncheck-spike-status.mjs\ncheck-version.sh\ncheck-version.test.ts\neslint-layers.test.ts\neslint-spec-docs.test.ts\nfixtures\nhelpers\nlib\nlore-list-tests.mjs\nlore-run-test.mjs\nrepoint-spec-anchors.mjs\nrepoint-spec-anchors.test.ts\nsmoke.sh\nverify-script.test.ts\n#!/usr/bin/env node\n// Emits the Lore test-command `list` shape: a single JSON array of\n// {id, name, file} objects, one per test, and nothing else on stdout.\n// Jest cannot resolve test names without executing them - { join, relative, resolve } from \"node:path\";\nimport process from \"node:process\";\nimport { findMisplacedCoverageLinks } from \"@re-cinq/eslint-plugin-re-lint/spec/spec-link-parser.js\";\nimport arg.startsWith(\"--\"));\nconst specArgs = args.filter((arg) => !arg.startsWith(\"--\"));\n\nfor (const flag of flags) {\n  if (flag === \"--json\") {\n    continue;\n  }\n  process.stderr.write(`${USAGE}\\n`);\n  process.exit(2);\n}\n\nconst asJson = flags.includes(\"--json\");\n\nconst discoverSpecs = () => {\n  const specsDir = join(root, \"specs\");\n  const slugs = readdirSync(specsDir, { withFileTypes: true })\n    .filter((entry) => entry.isDirectory())\n    .map((entry) => entry.name)\n    .sort();\n\n  return [...slugs.map((slug) => `specs/${slug}/spec.md`), \".specify/spec.md\"];\n};\n\nconst specPaths =\n  specArgs.length > 0\n    ? specArgs.map((path) => relative(root, resolve(root, path)))\n    : discoverSpecs();\n\nconst readSpec = (specPath) => {\n }\n};\n\nconst findings = [];\nconst specsWithFindings = new Set();\nlet statementsScanned = 0;\n\nfor (const specPath of specPaths) {\n  const content = readSpec(specPath);\n\n  for (const statement statement.line ?? null,\n        path: link.path,\n        anchorLine: link.line,\n        label: link.label,\n        statement: statement.text,\n      });\n      specsWithFindings.add(specPath);\n {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01LXVrQZ2Z8aqYS7g6fiCHYA","type":"tool_result","content":"#!/usr/bin/env node\n// Holds every `([validated by: <test name>](path#Lnn))` citation to the test it names.\n//\n// Usage:\n//   node scripts/check-spec-anchor-names.mjs           # report, exit 1 on any finding\n//   node scripts/check-spec-anchor-names.mjs --fix     # repoint each line from the name it carries\n//\n// A line number is the wrong thing to cite: inserting an import above a suite moves every test below\n// it, and the citation silently lands on a different, still-valid declaration. The name is what the\n// author meant, so the name is what the citation records and the line becomes a derived pointer this\n// script repairs. The `#Lnn` stays because the lint rule and the coverage job both index by it, and a\n// citation without one marks the whole file covered rather than one test.\nimport {readFileSync, writeFileSync} from 'node:fs';\nimport {execFileSync} from 'node:child_process';\nimport process from 'node:process';\nimport {dirname, join, normalize} from 'node:path';\nimport {root} from './lib/repo-root.mjs';\n\nconst CITATION = /\\[validated by(?<label>: [^\\]]*)?\\]\\((?<path>(?:\\.\\.\\/)+[\\w./-]+?)#L(?<line>\\d+)\\)/g;\n// Any other href shape is refused rather than ignored: the coverage job resolves `./../x` and `/x`\n// to the same file and counts them, while this gate and the drift check would never have read them.\nconst ANY_HREF = /\\[validated by(?:: [^\\]]*)?\\]\\((?<path>[^)#\\s]+)#L\\d+\\)/g;\n// `xit`, `xdescribe` and `.skip` declare a test that never runs, which validates nothing.\nconst DECLARATION = /^\\s*(?:f?it|f?describe|test)\\(\\s*(['\"`])((?:\\\\.|(?!\\1).)*)\\1/;\nconst SKIPPED = /^\\s*(?:x(?:it|describe|test)|(?:it|describe|test)\\.skip)\\(\\s*(['\"`])((?:\\\\.|(?!\\1).)*)\\1/;\nconst TEST_FILE = /\\.(?:test|spec)\\.[cm]?[jt]sx?$/;\n\nconst cache = new Map();\nconst args = process.argv.slice(2);\nconst fix = args.includes('--fix');\nconst named = args.filter(arg => !arg.startsWith('--'));\nconst findings = [];\nlet checked = 0;\nlet repaired = 0;\n\nfor (const spec of named.length > 0 ? named : specs()) {\n  const source = readFileSync(join(root, spec), 'utf8');\n  const specDir = dirname(spec);\n  let changed = false;\n\n  for (const match of source.matchAll(ANY_HREF)) {\n    if (!match.groups.path.startsWith('../')) {\n      findings.push(`${spec}: href \"${match.groups.path}\" must be relative and start with ../`);\n    }\n  }\n\n  const rewritten = source.replace(CITATION, (whole, label, path, line, ...rest) => {\n    const groups = rest[rest.length - 1];\n    const target = normalize(join(specDir, groups.path));\n    if (!TEST_FILE.test(target)) return whole;\n\n    checked += 1;\n    const declarations = declarationsIn(target);\n    if (declarations === null) {\n      findings.push(`${spec}: ${groups.path} does not exist`);\n      return whole;\n    }\n\n    const named = groups.label ? groups.label.slice(2) : null;\n    const atLine = declarations.find(declaration => declaration.line === Number(groups.line));\n\n    const skipped = declarations.find(d => d.skipped && (d.name === named || d.line === Number(groups.line)));\n    if (skipped) {\n      findings.push(`${spec}: cites \"${skipped.name}\" at ${groups.path}#L${skipped.line}, which is skipped and validates nothing`);\n      return whole;\n    }\n\n    if (named === null) {\n      if (!atLine) {\n        findings.push(`${spec}: ${groups.path}#L${groups.line} is not a declaration, so no name can be read from it`);\n        return whole;\n      }\n      if (nameIsAmbiguous(declarations, atLine.name)) {\n        findings.push(ambiguity(spec, groups.path, atLine.name, declarations));\n        return whole;\n      }\n      if (!fix) {\n        findings.push(`${spec}: ${groups.path}#L${groups.line} carries no test name`);\n        return whole;\n      }","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"085eaee9-e9f7-4fcf-ace0-bd2967c4b194","uuid":"cdc20694-f3f1-4b3c-aafd-3c4f71fa4f2b","timestamp":"2026-11-07T00:47:15.546Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"#!/usr/bin/env node\n// Holds every `([validated by: <test name>](path#Lnn))` citation to the test it names.\n//\n// Usage:\n//   node scripts/check-spec-anchor-names.mjs           # report, exit 1 on any finding\n//   node scripts/check-spec-anchor-names.mjs --fix     # repoint each line from the name it carries\n//\n// A line number is the wrong thing to cite: inserting an import above a suite moves every test below\n// it, and the citation silently lands on a different, still-valid declaration. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01FXtygxL5d3Ku4qgcW4PQsd","type":"tool_result","content":"# AGENTS.md\n\n## Context Loading Order\n\nAgents should read files in this order to understand the HAL Engine codebase:\n\n1. **README.md** - Overview, features, quick start, and project structure\n2. **package.json** - Dependencies, scripts, tech stack validation\n3. **tsconfig.json** - TypeScript configuration and compilation targets\n4. **src/index.ts** - Public API exports and module surface\n5. **src/config.ts** - Main factory function `createHalEngine()` and configuration schema\n6. **src/types/** - Core type definitions (ai.ts, session.ts, messages.ts, auth.ts, sessionStore.ts)\n7. **src/providers/** - AI provider implementations (bedrock, vertex, openai, anthropic, mock)\n8. **src/infrastructure/** - Support layers (builders, stores, parsers)\n9. **src/orchestration/** - Business logic (chatOrchestrator, tools, context management)\n10. **src/transport/** - Server setup (Express app, WebSocket, routes)\n11. **specs/** - Normative specs (architecture, WebSocket protocol, providers, tool responses)\n12. **adrs/** - Architecture Decision Records\n13. **docs/** - How-to guides, coding practices, and research spikes under docs/spikes/\n14. **.specify/spec.md** - Repo-level system spec (exempt from the spec header table convention)\n\n## Workflow Commands\n\n### Development\n```bash\nnpm run dev          # Start example/server.ts with hot reload (tsx watch)\nnpm run typecheck    # Run TypeScript type checking without emitting\n```\n\n### Building\n```bash\nnpm run build        # Compile TypeScript to dist/ (tsc)\nnpm start            # Run compiled JavaScript from dist/index.js\n```\n\n### Testing\n```bash\nnpm test             # Run Jest test suite\n# Tests use ts-jest for TypeScript compilation\n# Configuration: jest.config.js\n```\n\n### Code Quality\n```bash\nnpm run eslint       # Run ESLint with max-warnings 0 (zero-tolerance)\nnpm run prettier     # Format all src/**/*.ts with Prettier\nnpm run prettier:check  # Check formatting without writing\n```\n\n### All Checks (Recommended Pre-Commit)\n```bash\nnpm run verify\n```\n\nThat is the blocking set `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `verify` job runs on every\npull request, in the same order, and `scripts/verify-script.test.ts` fails if the\ntwo stop agreeing - so this command cannot quietly become a subset of what CI\ndemands. Two gates in that job are missing from it because both diff against\n`origin/main` and neither runs the same way on a checkout: the spec anchor check\nand the changelog check.\n\nTwo further CI jobs are worth running locally before a release or a change that\ntouches packaging: `npm run check:build-chain` (audit and action pins) and\n`npm run smoke` (packs the tarball and installs it outside this tree).\n\n## Spec Header Table\n\nEvery `specs/*/spec.md` opens with its title, then a two-column header table,\nand carries a lead paragraph of prose before its first `##` section:\n\n```markdown\n# <spec title>\n\n| Field  | Value                  |\n| ------ | ---------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#<n> |\n| Status | Draft                  |\n\n<lead paragraph>\n```\n\nThe lead paragraph does not have to sit immediately after the table: a spec\ncarrying a metadata block of its own may place that block between the two. What\nthe convention requires is that prose, rather than a section heading, follows\nthe header material, and that the prose runs to at least 40 characters -\nanything shorter is reported as no lead paragraph at all.\n\n`Issue` names the issue the spec was written against, or `n/a` for the specs\nthat predate this convention.\n\n`Status` is one of `Draft`, `In Progress` or `Shipped`. It is not a mood, and\nit is not a claim about how finished the feature is: it is the spec's own\ntest-citation coverage. No testable statement cited is `Draft`, some cited is\n`In Progress`, all cited is `Shipped`. A spec describing shipped, working\nbehaviour still reads `Draft` until its statements cite the tests that\nvalidate them.\n\nA spec that should no longer be graded takes a terminal value instead. The\nparser buckets roughly sixteen labels into five buckets, and the two terminal\nones skip the coverage check whatever the citation count:","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"085eaee9-e9f7-4fcf-ace0-bd2967c4b194","uuid":"c3a76386-6674-4356-9ba2-203ea3de886a","timestamp":"2026-11-07T00:47:22.347Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"# AGENTS.md\n\n## Context Loading Order\n\nAgents should read files in this order to understand the HAL Engine codebase:\n\n1. **README.md** -
