AI provider implementations (bedrock, vertex, openai, anthropic, mock)\n8. **src/infrastructure/** - Support layers (builders, stores, parsers)\n9. **src/orchestration/** - Business logic (chatOrchestrator, tools, context management)\n10. **src/transport/** - Server setup (Express app, WebSocket, routes)\n11. **specs/** - Normative specs (architecture, WebSocket protocol, providers, tool responses)\n12. **adrs/** - Architecture Decision Records\n13. **docs/** - How-to guides, coding practices, and research spikes under docs/spikes/\n14. **.specify/spec.md** - Repo-level system spec (exempt from the spec header table convention)\n\n## Workflow Commands\n\n### Development\n```bash\nnpm run dev          # Start example/server.ts with hot reload (tsx watch)\nnpm run typecheck    # Run TypeScript type checking without emitting\n```\n\n### Building\n```bash\nnpm run build        # Compile TypeScript to dist/ (tsc)\nnpm start            # Run compiled JavaScript from dist/index.js\n```\n\n### Testing\n```bash\nnpm test             # Run Jest test suite\n# Tests use ts-jest for TypeScript compilation\n# Configuration: jest.config.js\n```\n\n### Code Quality\n```bash\nnpm run eslint       # Run ESLint with max-warnings 0 (zero-tolerance)\nnpm run prettier     # Format all src/**/*.ts with Prettier\nnpm run prettier:check  # Check formatting without writing\n```\n\n### All Checks (Recommended Pre-Commit)\n```bash\nnpm run verify\n```\n\nThat is the blocking set `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `verify` job runs on every\npull request, in the same order, and `scripts/verify-script.test.ts` fails if the\ntwo stop agreeing - so this command cannot quietly become a subset of what CI\ndemands. Two gates in that job are missing from it because both diff against\n`origin/main` and neither runs the same way on a checkout: the spec anchor check\nand the changelog check.\n\nTwo further CI jobs are worth running locally before a release or a change that\ntouches packaging: `npm run check:build-chain` (audit and action pins) and\n`npm run smoke` (packs the tarball and installs it outside this tree).\n\n## Spec Header Table\n\nEvery `specs/*/spec.md` opens with its title, then a two-column header table,\nand carries a lead paragraph of prose before its first `##` section:\n\n```markdown\n# <spec title>\n\n| Field  | Value                  |\n| ------ | ---------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#<n> |\n| Status | Draft                  |\n\n<lead paragraph>\n```\n\nThe lead paragraph does not have to sit immediately after the table: a spec\ncarrying a metadata block of its own may place that block between the two. What\nthe convention requires is that prose, rather than a section heading, follows\nthe header material, and that the prose runs to at least 40 characters -\nanything shorter is reported as no lead paragraph at all.\n\n`Issue` names the issue the spec was written against, or `n/a` for the specs\nthat predate this convention.\n\n`Status` is one of `Draft`, `In Progress` or `Shipped`. It is not a mood, and\nit is not a claim about how finished the feature is: it is the spec's own\ntest-citation coverage. No testable statement cited is `Draft`, some cited is\n`In Progress`, all cited is `Shipped`. A spec describing shipped, working\nbehaviour still reads `Draft` until its statements cite the tests that\nvalidate them.\n\nA spec that should no longer be graded takes a terminal value instead. The\nparser buckets roughly sixteen labels into five buckets, and the two terminal\nones skip the coverage check whatever the citation count:","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01FXtygxL5d3Ku4qgcW4PQsd","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01NmJPp9ZPM5BUdT3KXv14h3","type":"tool_result","content":"100\tones skip the coverage check whatever the citation count:\n101\t\n102\t| Written into the spec                                            | Bucket                       |\n103\t| ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |\n104\t| `Draft`                                                          | `draft`                      |\n105\t| `In Progress`, `In Review`, `Planning`, `WIP`, `Proposed`        | `in-progress`                |\n106\t| `Shipped`, `Implemented`, `Complete`, `Accepted`, `Done`, `Live` | `shipped`                    |\n107\t| `Retired`, `Superseded`, `Removed`, `Deprecated`, `Obsolete`     | `retired` - skips the check  |\n108\t| `Rejected`, `Abandoned`                                          | `rejected` - skips the check |\n109\t\n110\tPrefer the canonical `Draft` / `In Progress` / `Shipped` for a live spec; reach\n111\tfor `Retired` or `Rejected` rather than leaving a superseded spec to claim a\n112\tcoverage tier it will never earn.\n113\t\n114\tADRs do not carry this table. They declare their state as YAML frontmatter\n115\t`status:` instead - `accepted` for a decision the code implements, `proposed`\n116\tfor one it does not. That field does mean maturity, which the spec `Status` row\n117\texplicitly does not. The difference is deliberate: ADRs are exempt from the\n118\tcoverage tier, because asking a decision record to cite a test for every\n119\tstatement it makes is the wrong ask of a decision record.\n120\t\n121\t`.specify/spec.md` is exempt from this section. It is the repo-level system\n122\tspec, it predates the convention, and its content is deliberately frozen -\n123\tgiving it a header table and a lead paragraph would mean writing new prose into\n124\ta file that is meant to stay unchanged. The exemption covers the header table\n125\tand the lead paragraph only: the Lore coverage job does scan\n126\t`.specify/spec.md`, so link placement still applies to it.\n127\t\n128\t## Spec Test Links\n129\t\n130\tStatements in `specs/*/spec.md` cite their validating test in a trailing\n131\tparenthetical at the end of the statement, where a list item counts as one\n132\tstatement and a paragraph counts per sentence. Tests live beside their source\n133\tin this repo, so the path points into `src/`:\n134\t\n135\t```markdown\n136\t- All entries within a single assistant response are committed before the next\n137\t  user message is processed\n138\t  ([validated by: commits every entry before the next user message](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L42)).\n139\t```\n140\t\n141\tThe citation carries the test's **name** as well as its line. The name is what the\n142\tauthor cited; the line is a derived pointer, and it goes stale on its own - adding\n143\tan import above a suite moves every test below it, and the citation lands on a\n144\tdifferent, still-valid declaration without anything noticing. `npm run spec:names`\n145\tholds each citation to the test it names, and `npm run spec:names:fix` repoints the\n146\tline from the name. The `#Lnn` stays because the lint rule and the coverage job\n147\tboth index by it; a citation without one marks the whole file covered rather than\n148\tone test.\n149\t\n150\tA citation counts as coverage only where it is trailing, and a test link placed\n151\tanywhere else in the statement is a misplaced citation rather than a weaker one.\n152\tUpstream `check:spec-links` reports it and exits 1, and the coverage job that\n153\tconsumes these specs records nothing for it - so a spec can read as fully cited while its coverage is\n154\tzero. A mid-statement link to something that is not a test file - a script, a\n155\tdoc, another spec - is ordinary prose, is never reported, and was never coverage\n156\tto begin with. A misplaced citation is fixed by moving it to the end of its\n157\tstatement, never by deleting it.\n158\t\n159\tIn a test file the `#Lnn` anchor must land on the `it(`, `test(` or `describe(`\n160\tdeclaration itself - not a blank line, not one holding only closing punctuation,\n161\tand not a line inside the test body. A citation onto an `expect` renders and\n162\tresolves like any other, and proves nothing: no reader can turn that line back\n163\tinto the name of a case. `scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs` reports all four as\n164\t`rotten` and exits 1 in both modes. A citation into a file that is not a test -\n165\ta workflow, a config, `package.json` - has no declaration to land on and is held\n166\tonly to existing and carrying content.\n167\t\n168\tWhat no gate can catch is an anchor aimed at the wrong declaration: a real but\n169\tunintended `it(` passes every check. The tell is that the test the statement\n170\tmeant to cite goes on reading as uncited in the `require-spec-link` backlog.\n171\t\n172\tAnchors drift silently whenever a cited file gains or loses lines above them, so\n173\tafter editing any cited file re-run `node scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs`\n174\trather than hand-correcting the numbers. That script cannot repair a spec absent\n175\tfrom the base ref, since it has no baseline to translate from; on the branch that\n176\tintroduces a spec, its anchors have to be corrected by hand.\n177\t\n178\tThe convention's tooling is wired up here: `npm run check:spec-links`, `npm run check:spec-status` and\n179\t`node scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs --check origin/main` all gate CI, and\n180\t`re-lint/require-status-matches-coverage` derives each `Status` row from its\n181\tspec's citations. `re-lint/require-spec-link` gates in `eslint.config.mjs`: every test cites a\n182\tstatement, so an uncited new test fails the build. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01MSo4UEXrS3w2AyzkgjhgDf","type":"tool_result","content":"/**\n * require-spec-link — every `it()`/`test()` must be linked to a spec or ADR.\n *\n * The spec-traceability convention authors the link INSIDE the spec.md/adr `.md`\n * as an inline trailing parenthetical — `Statement. ([validated by](test.ts#L42))`.\n * This rule inverts that view: it goes over each test declaration and fails the\n * ones that no spec or ADR statement references, so tests cannot silently drift\n * out of spec coverage.\n *\n * A test is linked when the spec/adr corpus carries a `([validated by](this#Lnn))`\n * link whose line falls inside the test's line span, or a whole-file link (no\n * `#L`) to the file. The corpus is read once per process (memoized per specsRoot)\n * via the shared parser — see `lib/spec-link-index.mjs`. Type info is off for\n * test files, so this works from the ESTree AST + filename + filesystem only.\n *\n * Detect-only: writing the link needs a human to pick which statement the test\n * validates, not a codemod.\n */\nimport path from \"node:path\";\nimport { isTestFile } from \"./lib/spec-parsers.mjs\";\nimport { buildLinkIndex, corpusExists, readSpecFiles, toPosix, } from \"./lib/spec-link-index.mjs\";\n/**\n * specsRoot+roots → index, so the corpus is walked once per process.\n *\n * Note: the cache is keyed on specsRoot only, with no mtime/content hash — a\n * single-pass `eslint`/CI run always reads fresh, but a long-lived ESLint\n * server (VS Code extension, --watch) will not see a spec link added after the\n * first lint until the server restarts.\n */\nconst indexCache = new Map();\n/** specsRoots we've already warned about having no spec/adr corpus, so a\n * misconfigured run warns once instead of once per linted test file.\n *\n * Scope is the whole Node process, matching `indexCache` above. In a one-shot\n * `eslint`/CI run that is exactly one warning. In a long-lived ESLint server\n * (VS Code, --watch) the module is not re-required between cycles, so once a\n * specsRoot has warned it stays quiet even after the CWD is fixed — the same\n * process-lifetime memoization caveat the corpus cache carries. Restart the\n * server (or the editor) to re-arm the warning. */\nconst warnedMissingCorpus = new Set();\nfunction getIndex(specsRoot, roots) {\n    // `\\0` separates every element so no root value can collide with the joiner.\n    const key = [specsRoot, ...roots].join(\"\\0\");\n    let index = indexCache.get(key);\n    if (!index) {\n        index = buildLinkIndex(readSpecFiles(specsRoot, roots));\n        indexCache.set(key, index);\n    }\n    return index;\n}\n/** The base identifier of a (possibly chained) callee — `it` in `it.each([])(…)`. */\nfunction rootCallName(callee) {\n    let node = callee;\n    while (node) {\n        if (node.type === \"Identifier\") {\n            return node.name;\n        }\n        if (node.type === \"MemberExpression\") {\n            node = node.object;\n            continue;\n        }\n        if (node.type === \"CallExpression\") {\n            node = node.callee;\n            continue;\n        }\n        // `it.each`table`(name, fn)` — the outer callee is a tagged template whose\n        // `.tag` is the `it.each` member chain; drill into it or the test escapes.\n        if (node.type === \"TaggedTemplateExpression\") {\n            node = node.tag;\n            continue;\n        }\n        return null;\n    }\n    return null;\n}\n/** A named test declaration: `it`/`test` (any modifier chain) with a string name. */\nfunction isNamedTestCall(node) {\n    const root = rootCallName(node.callee);\n    if (root !== \"it\" && root !== \"test\") {\n        return false;\n    }\n    const first = node.arguments[0];\n    if (!first) {\n        return false;\n    }\n    if (first.type === \"Literal\") {\n        return typeof first.value === \"string\";\n    }\n    return first.type === \"TemplateLiteral\";\n}\nfunction isLinked(entry, startLine, endLine) {\n    if (!entry) {\n        return false;\n    }\n    if (entry.wholeFile) {\n        return true;\n    }\n    for (const line of entry.lines) {\n        if (line >= startLine && line <= endLine) {\n            return true;","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"085eaee9-e9f7-4fcf-ace0-bd2967c4b194","uuid":"b1c056d9-ed1c-4e6a-a2c3-d02a3b75dabf","timestamp":"2026-11-07T00:47:45.539Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"/**\n * require-spec-link — every `it()`/`test()` must be linked to a spec or ADR.\n *\n * The spec-traceability convention authors the link INSIDE the spec.md/adr `.md`\n * as an inline trailing parenthetical — `Statement. Restart the\n * server (or the editor) to re-arm the warning. */\nconst warnedMissingCorpus = new Set();\nfunction getIndex(specsRoot, roots) {\n    // `\\0` separates every element so no root value can collide with the joiner.\n    const key = [specsRoot, ...roots].join(\"\\0\");\n    let index = indexCache.get(key);\n    if (!index) {\n        index = buildLinkIndex(readSpecFiles(specsRoot, roots));\n        indexCache.set(key, index);\n    }\n    return index;\n}\n/** The base identifier of a (possibly chained) callee — `it` in `it.each([])(…)`. */\nfunction rootCallName(callee) {\n    let node = callee;\n    while (node) {\n        if (node.type === \"Identifier\") {\n            return node.name;\n        }\n        if (node.type === \"MemberExpression\") {\n            node = node.object;\n            continue;\n        }\n        if (node.type === \"CallExpression\") {\n            node = node.callee;\n            continue;\n        }\n        // `it.each`table`(name, fn)` — the outer callee is a tagged template whose\n        // `.tag` is the `it.each` member chain; drill into it or the test escapes.\n        if (node.type === \"TaggedTemplateExpression\") {\n            node = node.tag;\n            continue;\n        }\n        return null;\n    }\n    return null;\n}\n/** A named test declaration: `it`/`test` (any modifier chain) with a string name. */\nfunction isNamedTestCall(node) {\n    const root = rootCallName(node.callee);\n    if (root !== \"it\" && root !== \"test\") {\n        return false;\n    }\n    const first = node.arguments[0];\n    if (!first) {\n        return false;\n    }\n    if (first.type === \"Literal\") {\n        return typeof first.value === \"string\";\n    }\n    return first.type === \"TemplateLiteral\";\n}\nfunction isLinked(entry, startLine, endLine) {\n    if (!entry) {\n        return false;\n    }\n    if (entry.wholeFile) {\n        return true;\n    }\n    for (const line of entry.lines) {\n        if (line >= startLine && line <= endLine) {\n            return true;","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01MSo4UEXrS3w2AyzkgjhgDf","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}
